#1196. UEF FLEX: 50 triệu đồng đầu tư thay đổi mô hình giáo dục đại học tại UEH

Đại học Kinh tế TP.HCM ra mắt mô hình UEF FLEX, đầu tư 50 triệu đồng mỗi dự án để thúc đẩy sinh viên khởi nghiệp thực chiến. Sáng kiến này xóa bỏ rào cản giữa lý thuyết và thị trường, giúp người học tạo ra sản phẩm thực tế đầy tiềm năng.

UEH Technology Convergence Hub chính thức ra mắt cùng dự án thí điểm UEF FLEX tại Đại học Kinh tế TP. Hồ Chí Minh, đánh dấu bước chuyển dịch mạnh mẽ trong mô hình giáo dục đại học với đầu tư khởi điểm 50 triệu đồng đối với mỗi dự án sinh viên. Trong bối cảnh nền kinh tế số và thị trường lao động đòi hỏi nguồn nhân lực có tư duy thực chiến cao, sự kiện ra mắt này không chỉ phản ánh tầm nhìn chiến lược của một trong những trung tâm đào tạo kinh tế hàng đầu Việt Nam, mà còn minh chứng cho xu hướng tích hợp sâu rộng giữa khoa học công nghệ, tri thức hàn lâm và nhu cầu thực tiễn của thị trường. Thay vì các khóa học truyền thống tĩnh định, nền tảng này tập trung vào việc kết nối tri thức, đổi mới sáng tạo và phát triển bền vững thông qua các dự án thực tế. Theo thống kê nội bộ, UEH nhận diện hơn 2.000 dự án thí điểm trong 6 tháng đầu tiên, với tỷ lệ chuyển đổi thành sản phẩm thương mại đạt 30% – con số này cho thấy tiềm năng lớn của mô hình kết hợp học tập với kinh doanh thực tế, đồng thời khẳng định năng lực thích ứng nhanh chóng của thế hệ sinh viên trẻ trước các biến động của nền kinh tế hiện đại tại TP. Hồ Chí Minh cũng như cả nước.

Dự án UEF FLEX hoạt động như một "cỗ máy" triệt tiêu rào cản giữa giáo dục và marketplace. Sinh viên không chỉ học lý thuyết trên giảng đường mà phải trực tiếp đưa ra sản phẩm hoặc dịch vụ ra mắt thị trường, chịu sự kiểm chứng khắt khe từ người tiêu dùng và nhận sự hướng dẫn sát sao từ các doanh nghiệp đối tác dày dặn kinh nghiệm. Cơ chế này đòi hỏi người học phải vận dụng toàn diện các kiến thức về quản trị, tài chính, tiếp thị và công nghệ để giải quyết những bài toán kinh doanh cụ thể, từ đó thu hẹp khoảng cách giữa nhà trường và doanh nghiệp. Ví dụ điển hình là nhóm sinh viên khoa Kế toán đã phát triển ứng dụng quản lý tài chính cho hợp tác xã nông nghiệp, qua đó không chỉ giúp tối ưu hóa quy trình vận hành cho khu vực kinh tế tập thể mà còn thu hút vốn từ nhà đầu tư áng sau 3 tháng thử nghiệm. Sự thành công bước đầu của những mô hình như vậy cho thấy sức sống mạnh mẽ của các ý tưởng xuất phát từ giảng đường, đồng thời phản ánh xu thế hợp tác đa ngành giữa UEH với các địa phương, đơn vị trong việc thúc đẩy hệ sinh thái khởi nghiệp đổi mới sáng tạo lan tỏa rộng khắp.

Tại sao UEH lại chọn số 50 triệu đồng chính xác cho mỗi dự án khởi điểm? Cơ cấu chi phí này không phải là một con số ngẫu nhiên mà được tính toán kỹ lưỡng, bao gồm chi phí phát triển phần mềm cơ bản, thiết kế mẫu sản phẩm và chi phí tiếp thị ban đầu – vừa đủ để một nhóm 3-4 sinh viên bắt đầu từ con số không mà không bị quá tải về gánh nặng tài chính. So sánh với các trường đại học khác tại TP. Hồ Chí Minh nơi chi phí khởi động dự án thường dao động từ 100 - 200 triệu đồng, UEH đặt ra mục tiêu tiếp cận sinh viên từ các tầng lớp tài chính trung bình, mở rộng cơ hội cho mọi người học thay vì chỉ dành cho nhóm giàu có hay có nền tảng gia đình khá giả. Đây là bước ngoặt quan trọng trong việc phổ biến hóa đổi mới sáng tạo tại Việt Nam, đảm bảo rằng tư duy kinh doanh và công nghệ không bị giới hạn bởi rào cản tiền tệ ban đầu. Việc hạ thấp ngưỡng cửa đầu tư này cũng tạo động lực tâm lý tích cực, khuyến khích sinh viên dám nghĩ, dám làm và chấp nhận thử thách trong một môi trường được hỗ trợ toàn diện bởi hệ sinh thái của trường.

Lộ trình triển khai cho thấy thay đổi sẽ được người học cảm nhận ngay từ kỳ học tiếp theo, đòi hỏi sự chuẩn bị kỹ lưỡng về thời gian và tâm thế từ phía sinh viên lẫn gia đình. Từ tháng 11/2026, tất cả các lớp năm nhất bắt buộc tham gia một dự án UEF FLEX ngắn hạn (6 tuần) để hoàn thành tín chỉ tốt nghiệp, biến hoạt động đổi mới sáng tạo thành một phần cốt lõi trong chương trình đào tạo chuẩn. Điều này đồng nghĩa với việc sinh viên phải dành thêm 4-5 tiếng mỗi tuần cho hoạt động thực hành, làm việc nhóm và khảo sát thị trường thay vì chỉ tập trung vào bài giảng lý thuyết trên lớp. Đối với các bậc phụ huynh, điều này có nghĩa là chi phí phụ trợ có thể tăng nhẹ do nhu cầu trang bị thiết bị kỹ thuật số hoặc chuẩn bị vốn dự án tối thiểu, nhưng bù lại, cơ hội việc làm sau đại học được tăng cường đáng kể nhờ portfolio dự án thực tế phong phú và uy tín chuyên môn được tích lũy ngay từ những năm đầu đại học.

Tập trung vào tiêu chí đổi mới sáng tạo tại UEH Technology Convergence Hub còn đi kèm với trách nhiệm sâu sắc về môi trường và phát triển bền vững. Trong bối cảnh chuyển đổi xanh đang trở thành ưu tiên chiến lược quốc gia, UEH tuyên bố sẽ ưu tiên các dự án có giải pháp xanh, giảm thiểu rác thải và thân thiện với sinh thái. Một dự án thí điểm gần đây từ nhóm sinh viên Khoa Thiết kế đồ họa đã tạo ra bao bì gói thực phẩm hoàn toàn phân hủy sinh học, giải quyết trực tiếp bài toán rác thải nhựa đang gây nhức nhối, và vinh dự nhận được chứng nhận xanh từ Bộ Công Thương vào tháng 10/2026. Đây là minh chứng rõ rệt cho việc khoa học công nghệ và tư duy kinh doanh không chỉ tạo ra lợi ích kinh tế ngắn hạn mà còn đáp ứng nghiêm ngặt các yêu cầu tiêu thụ xanh đang gia tăng mạnh mẽ tại thị trường nội địa, đồng thời định hướng cho sinh viên ý thức về trách nhiệm xã hội trong từng sản phẩm thương mại.

Câu hỏi lớn nhất hiện tại của cộng đồng giáo dục là liệu mô hình kết hợp giữa nguồn vốn khởi điểm tối ưu và thực chiến thị trường này có thể lan rộng ra khắp các trường đại học Việt Nam hay không. Với cơ cấu chi phí khởi điểm chỉ 50 triệu đồng và kết quả chuyển đổi ấn tượng 30% thành sản phẩm thương mại thực tế, UEH hoàn toàn có thể trở thành tiêu chuẩn de facto cho giáo dục đại học thế hệ mới trong việc gắn kết nhà trường với nền kinh tế thị trường. Tuy nhiên, thách thức lớn nằm ở việc duy trì chất lượng giáo lý lý thuyết song song với hoạt động thực hành – nếu sinh viên chỉ tập trung vào "đoán thị trường" ngắn hạn, điều đó có thể làm méo mó nền tảng kiến thức chuyên sâu mang tính hàn lâm. Câu trả lời xác đáng nhất sẽ nằm ở cách nhà trường điều chỉnh, cân bằng chương trình đào tạo trong 3 năm tới, để đảm bảo sự hài hòa tuyệt đối giữa kỹ năng mềm, tư duy kinh doanh thực chiến và chuyên môn cứng của người học.

#1197. Geopolitical Flashpoints: Gulf States Warned War Could Extend Past Year as Oil Markets React

As a Trump administration envoy predicts the Iran conflict could persist for another twelve months and global oil prices climb, fresh drone activity over Beirut and a Saudi-led coalition intercepting overnight attacks signal a rapidly escalating Middle East theater with profound global energy and security stakes.

Why the Strait of Hormuz Chokes 20% of the World's Oil

The global energy market hangs by a precarious thread through the Strait of Hormuz, where roughly 20% of the world's petroleum liquids transit daily. Recent remarks from a senior Trump administration envoy have sharpened fears that this vital waterway could become a theater of prolonged conflict. In a CBS News report published on October 23, 2026, the envoy warned that the war with Iran could last another year, a projection that directly correlates with climbing oil prices and heightened risk premiums embedded in global benchmarks. The strait's narrow geography — at its narrowest point just 21 miles wide, with only two miles allocated for shipping traffic — makes it uniquely vulnerable to disruption. Any sustained exchange of fire, mining of the channel, or blockade would not only spike prices at the pump but could trigger broader economic slowdowns in Asia and Europe already grappling with inflationary pressures. The Trump administration's dual track of maximal pressure and potential diplomatic off-ramp has left Gulf states recalibrating their security postures, with several increasing naval patrols and investing in alternative energy pipelines to mitigate exposure. As the envoy's forecast settles into the regional calculus, the question is no longer if the strait will face testing, but when and at what cost to the global economy.

Drone Incursions over Beirut Signal Hezbollah Escalation

Parallel to the energy flashpoint, the Lebanese capital Beirut has become a frontline for aerial activity that many analysts interpret as a prelude to broader escalation with Hezbollah. The New Arab reported on the same day that Israeli drones were observed circling and overflying Beirut's southern suburbs, areas traditionally strongholds of the Iranian-backed militia. While the Israeli military has maintained a policy of ambiguity regarding specific operations, the frequency and persistence of these incursions suggest a deliberate effort to degrade Hezbollah's command-and-control infrastructure or gather real-time intelligence ahead of potential kinetic action. For Hezbollah, which has spent years fortifying its positions along the border and within Lebanese cities, such aerial pressure compounds an already tense domestic situation following years of economic collapse and the 2020 port explosion. The group's leadership has repeatedly warned that any aggression on Lebanese soil will be met with a response that could draw in wider regional actors, including Iran itself. The drone activity, therefore, is not merely a tactical probe but a signal that the rules of engagement are shifting, and that the northern front could flare at any moment, with consequences for civilian populations caught in the crosshairs.

Saudi-Led Coalition Intercepts Wave of Overnight Attacks

Yemen's complex civil war, now in its tenth year, continues to reverberate across the Arabian Peninsula, with the Saudi-led coalition reporting the interception of a fresh wave of overnight attacks. The National's morning briefing on October 23 highlighted that coalition air defenses successfully engaged multiple projectiles launched toward Saudi territory, a routine but increasingly dangerous occurrence that underscores the conflict's endurance. The attacks, claimed by Ansar Allah — the Houthi movement — target critical infrastructure and energy facilities, aiming to pressure the kingdom's oil-dependent economy and test the resolve of the coalition's air defense networks. In recent months, the Houthis have expanded their reach, occasionally threatening commercial shipping in the Red Sea and Bab el-Mandeb, further complicating maritime security. The coalition's intercepts, while preventing casualties and major damage, reveal the limitations of current defense postures against sustained drone and missile barrages. For the Yemeni population, the cycle of bombardment and retaliation exacts a heavy humanitarian toll, with displaced persons, disrupted supply lines, and the ever-present threat of famine in areas already struggling under blockades. The international community, including the United Nations, has called for a return to negotiated solutions, but the battlefield dynamics on the ground suggest any ceasefire remains fragile and easily violated.

Trump's Strategic Calculus: War vs. Letting the Conflict Wither

Back in the United States, the incoming administration's approach to the Iran conundrum is playing out in real time, with competing impulses on display. Reuters reported that President Trump is weighing a quick end to the conflict against allowing it to "wither" on the vine, a strategic dilemma that has profound implications for both Middle East stability and the global energy market. On one hand, a rapid diplomatic breakthrough — potentially involving sanctions relief in exchange for nuclear concessions — could stabilize oil prices and reduce the risk of a broader war. On the other, a strategy of "letting the conflict wither" risks prolonging the period of uncertainty, during which oil markets will continue to price in risk, Iranian proxies may grow bolder, and U.S. allies in the Gulf may feel compelled to pursue independent security arrangements. The Trump envoy's year-long war projection, therefore, sits at the intersection of these two paths: it acknowledges the military reality that neither side can achieve a decisive victory quickly, while also signaling that the administration is prepared to sustain pressure campaigns for an extended period. This calculus has not gone unnoticed by Gulf capitals, which are hedging their bets by deepening defense ties with both Washington and Beijing, and by European powers seeking to re-engage diplomatically while maintaining sanctions pressure.

Beyond the immediate military and economic metrics, the sustained trajectory of the Iran conflict is generating severe humanitarian and regional ripple effects that extend well beyond the immediate battlefield. In Iran itself, the population contends with the dual burden of economic sanctions and the redirection of national resources toward military and proxy expenditures. The year-long war forecast exacerbates domestic discontent, particularly among working-class and younger demographics already strained by inflation and unemployment. Across the border in Iraq, the country finds itself caught between the competing influences of Washington and Tehran, with political factions aligned to each patron jockeying for position in a fragile parliamentary system. The risk of spillover into Syria remains high, where Iranian-backed militias and Turkish forces operate in close proximity, any clash of which could draw in Russia and the United States. For the Gulf Cooperation Council states, the imperative of diversification — both economic and security-wise — has accelerated, with massive investments in renewable energy, water desalination, and joint military initiatives aimed at reducing reliance on a security umbrella that may prove transient. The cumulative effect of these dynamics is a region in flux, where the stakes of every military move are amplified by the interdependence of energy, politics, and human security.

International Responses and the Path Ahead

The international response to the converging crises in the Middle East reflects a fragmented landscape of interests and red lines. European powers, while united in their condemnation of Iranian aggression, have been vocal about the need to avoid a complete rupture that could send oil prices spiraling and destabilize global markets. The United Nations has reiterated its offer of good offices for de-escalation, though past rounds of talks have yielded limited progress, often stymied by mutual distrust and the absence of a comprehensive political framework. Meanwhile, regional actors such as Qatar and Oman have continued to serve as back-channel mediators, seeking to bridge gaps between Tehran and Riyadh. As the year unfolds, several scenarios merit close watching: a negotiated framework that freezes certain nuclear activities in exchange for limited sanctions relief; a miscalculation that triggers a direct exchange between Iran and Israel, drawing in U.S. forces; or a gradual normalization of relations between Saudi Arabia and Israel, mediated by Washington, that sidelines the Iranian issue temporarily but leaves the underlying rivalry intact. The common thread across all scenarios is the enduring centrality of the Persian Gulf to global stability, and the reality that the choices made in Washington, Tehran, and Riyadh will shape the security architecture of the decades to come.

#1198. Gulf in Flux: Hormuz Straits, Shifting Alliances and the Shadow of Renewed Conflict

As President Trump warns of a renewed "Epic Fury" scenario in the Persian Gulf, the strategic landscape of the Middle East shifts dramatically with new naval drills, evolving ceasefire dynamics in Yemen, and realigning great-power interests threatening global energy security.

Why the Strait of Hormuz Chokes 20% of the World's Oil

The Persian Gulf’s strategic choke point, the Strait of Hormuz, remains the linchpin of global energy security, through which roughly 20% of the world's oil and nearly all liquefied natural gas exports from Qatar pass daily. As of late October 2026, the waterway is witnessing a marked escalation in military posturing that underscores its fragility. According to recent reports, the United States Navy has increased its presence in the region, conducting freedom of navigation operations aimed at countering perceived threats from Iran. This heightened activity comes amid warnings from Iranian officials that any disruption to their oil exports would trigger a response capable of closing the strait, a scenario that could send global oil prices spiraling. The strait's narrow width—just 21 miles at its narrowest point—means that any military engagement, even a localized one, has the potential to disrupt supplies for the world's largest economies, from Europe to Asia. The current environment is further complicated by the presence of commercial vessels navigating these waters, which must now contend with the risk of accidental clashes or intentional harassment by state and non-state actors alike.

Trump's "Epic Fury" Warning and the Return of Maximum Pressure

President Donald Trump’s recent remarks warning of "another little Epic Fury" have injected a new level of volatility into an already tense region. The "Epic Fury" reference appears to harken back to the maximum pressure campaign of the previous administration, which sought to isolate Iran economically and militarily. Trump’s warning suggests a return to a policy of aggressive deterrence, potentially signaling that the U.S. is prepared to escalate military commitments if Iran continues its uranium enrichment activities or supports proxy operations. This rhetoric is not merely political posturing; it reflects a recalibration of U.S. strategic priorities in the Middle East, where the administration appears willing to accept higher risks of conflict to prevent what it views as Iranian aggression. The warning has been met with cautious analysis from regional experts, who note that while the language is bellicose, the actual policy shifts may be more nuanced, focusing on strengthening regional partners like Saudi Arabia and the UAE while maintaining a delicate balance with Iran to avoid a full-scale war that could destabilize the entire Middle East.

Iran's Drift from the Nuclear Deal and Regional Reactions

Iran’s nuclear program has advanced significantly beyond the limits set by the 2015 Joint Comprehensive Plan of Action (JCPOA), a development that has profound implications for regional and global security. As of the latest reporting, Iran has enriched uranium to levels approaching 60%, a short technical step away from weapons-grade material of 90%. This rapid progression comes as indirect talks between Tehran and world powers have effectively stalled, leaving a diplomatic vacuum that has been filled by military posturing and regional proxy activity. Neighboring Gulf states, particularly Saudi Arabia and the United Arab Emirates, are watching these developments with increasing apprehension. Riyadh has indicated that it may reconsider its own security architecture and potentially pursue indigenous nuclear capabilities if Iran's breakout capacity continues to grow. The international community, led by the European signatories to the JCPOA, has expressed deep concern, urging restraint and a return to dialogue, but the window for diplomatic resolution appears to be narrowing as both sides entrench their positions.

The Houthi Threat and the Yemen Ceasefire’s Fragile State

In Yemen, the Iran-backed Houthi rebels have continued their campaign of cross-border attacks against Saudi territory, even as a UN-brokered truce has nominally held since late 2023. Recent weeks have seen an uptick in drone and missile launches targeting Saudi infrastructure, which Riyadh has intercepted, but the underlying conflict remains unresolved. The Houthis have justified these actions as support for Palestinians in the Gaza Strip, linking the Yemeni conflict to the broader regional fallout from the ongoing Israel-Hamas war. This linkage complicates any efforts to isolate the Yemeni conflict, as it becomes entangled with the wider Palestinian issue and the strategic calculations of Iran. The humanitarian situation in Yemen remains dire, with millions dependent on aid, and the continued militarization of the border threatens to push the country back into full-scale war. Regional actors, including Oman and Qatar, are playing mediating roles, but the prospects for a durable peace remain slim as long as the broader regional rivalry between Iran and the Saudi-led coalition persists.

Great-Power Rivalries and the Redrawing of Alliances

Beyond the immediate actors of Iran and the Gulf Arab states, the current flashpoint is being shaped by the broader great-power rivalry between the United States, China, and Russia. China’s growing economic influence in the region, particularly through the Belt and Road Initiative, has positioned Beijing as a key player in any future energy or security arrangements. Meanwhile, Russia has maintained a strong relationship with Iran, providing military equipment and diplomatic support that bolsters Tehran’s position against the U.S. and its allies. This triangulation of interests means that any escalation in the Gulf is unlikely to remain contained; it could trigger a broader response involving naval assets from multiple powers. The recent joint naval drills between Iran and Russia further signal a deepening of this partnership, potentially altering the balance of power in the Mediterranean and the Gulf. For the U.S., the challenge lies in maintaining its traditional security commitments to Gulf allies while navigating a complex web of diplomatic and military relationships with rival powers who have their own strategic interests in the region.

The Humanitarian and Economic Ripple Effects

The consequences of the current Gulf flashpoint extend far beyond the immediate theater of military operations, touching on global humanitarian and economic systems. A disruption of Hormuz traffic would not only spike energy prices but also exacerbate inflationary pressures in economies already struggling with post-pandemic recovery. Developing nations, which are most vulnerable to energy cost shocks, could face heightened food insecurity as transport costs for goods rise. The psychological impact on seafarers and coastal communities, who live with the constant threat of conflict, adds a human dimension to the geopolitical calculations. International humanitarian organizations have warned that any renewed conflict could disrupt aid flows to already fragile regions, including Yemen and the Horn of Africa. The interconnectedness of these risks means that policymakers must balance the immediate security imperatives of the Gulf with the longer-term goal of global economic stability and humanitarian protection.

What to Watch Next: Scenarios and Flashpoints

Looking ahead, several key scenarios are worth monitoring as the situation evolves. First, the possibility of a deliberate Iranian move to close the Strait of Hormuz in response to renewed sanctions or military action remains the most acute flashpoint, one that could trigger a rapid and severe international response. Second, the trajectory of the U.S.-Iran nuclear talks—currently at a standstill—could either lead to a gradual de-escalation if both sides return to the negotiating table or a dangerous acceleration of enrichment that narrows the path to diplomacy. Third, the internal politics of Gulf states, particularly Saudi Arabia’s succession planning and its security relationship with the U.S., will shape the region's defensive postures. Finally, the role of non-state actors, including the Houthis and other proxy groups, will determine whether the conflict remains a bilateral U.S.-Iran confrontation or expands into a broader regional war. Analysts suggest that the coming months will be critical in determining whether the region moves toward a precarious but manageable status quo or slides into a conflict with unpredictable and far-reaching consequences.

#1199. สกายวอล์คเขาเขื่อนลั่น 50 ล้านบาท กับคำถามถึงความคุ้มค่าและความยั่งยืน

การสร้างสกายวอล์คเขาเขื่อนลั่นจังหวัดนครราชสีมาด้วยงบประมาณกว่า 50 ล้านบาท กำลังถูกตั้งคำถามถึงความคุ้มค่าและความยั่งยืนในระยะยาว เนื่องจากเป็นการลงทุนสร้างแลนด์มาร์คใหม่แทนการพัฒนาโครงสร้างพื้นฐานเดิม...

การเปิดตัวสกายวอล์คเขาเขื่อนลั่นในจังหวัดนครราชสีมาด้วยงบประมาณรัฐกว่า 50 ล้านบาท ไม่ใช่เพียงการสร้างแลนด์มาร์คใหม่เพื่อกระตุ้นเศรษฐกิจในพื้นที่ แต่สะท้อนให้เห็นถึงความพยายามของภาครัฐที่เร่งอัดฉีดงบประมาณลงสู่โครงการเชิงสัญลักษณ์ท่ามกลางข้อกังขาเรื่องความยั่งยืน การทุ่มงบประมาณมหาศาลพัฒนาแหล่งท่องเที่ยวในลักษณะ "สร้างใหม่" แทนการปรับปรุงโครงสร้างพื้นฐานเดิม กำลังเป็นความเสี่ยงเชิงนโยบายที่อาจสร้างภาระงบประมาณระยะยาวมากกว่าผลตอบแทนทางเศรษฐกิจที่แท้จริง

เมื่อวิเคราะห์ผ่านบริบทความผันผวนของเศรษฐกิจไทยในปัจจุบัน บรรดานักลงทุนและนักวิเคราะห์ต่างมองหาความมั่นคงในกลุ่มธุรกิจที่มีความแข็งแกร่ง เช่นเดียวกับที่ บล.เอเซีย พลัส (ASPS) ได้คัดเลือก 11 หุ้นเด่นใน 3 กลุ่มแกร่ง ได้แก่ "โรงไฟฟ้า-รพ.-ท่องเที่ยว" เพื่อใช้เป็นเกราะหลบภัยท่ามกลางตลาดหุ้นไทยที่ผันผวน ซึ่งสะท้อนว่าภาคธุรกิจจริง ๆ เน้นความยั่งยืนและการบริหารความเสี่ยงอย่างรอบคอบ ตรงกันข้ามกับการใช้จ่ายงบประมาณภาครัฐบางโครงการที่อาจยังขาดการประเมินความคุ้มค่าในระยะยาวอย่างรอบด้าน นโบลายการอัดฉีดเม็ดเงินลงสู่โครงการก่อสร้างโครงสร้างพื้นฐานด้านการท่องเที่ยวจึงต้องถูกตั้งคำถามถึงความเหมาะสมในการจัดสรรทรัพยากรท่ามกลางความท้าทายรอบด้าน

กรมการท่องเที่ยวได้อนุมัติงบประมาณกว่า 50 ล้านบาท เพื่อพัฒนาสกายวอล์คเขาเขื่อนลั่น โดยตั้งเป้าให้เป็นจุดเชื่อมต่อกับเส้นทางท่องเที่ยว Geo Route ในจังหวัดนครราชสีมา ข้อมูลจากแหล่งข่าวระบุว่าโครงการนี้ถูกผลักดันอย่างเร่งด่วนเพื่อเปิดรับนักท่องเที่ยวในช่วงปลายปี 2569 โดยมีการเปิดให้เข้าชมฟรีจนถึงวันที่ 30 ตุลาคม 2569 เท่านั้น แม้ภาพลักษณ์ภายนอกจะเป็นการส่งเสริมการท่องเที่ยวเชิงนิเวศ แต่คำถามสำคัญที่ยังไม่มีคำตอบชัดเจนจากหน่วยงานที่เกี่ยวข้องคือ แผนการบำรุงรักษาหลังจากสิ้นสุดช่วงโปรโมชั่นฟรีนี้จะเป็นอย่างไร

เมื่อพิจารณาจากกรณีศึกษาโครงการลักษณะเดียวกันในอดีต พบว่าหลายแห่งประสบปัญหาขาดงบประมาณดูแลรักษาจนกลายเป็น "ของตาย" หลังผ่านไปเพียงไม่กี่ปี ตัวเลข 50 ล้านบาทนี้เมื่อเทียบกับโครงสร้างพื้นฐานรอบข้างที่ยังต้องการการพัฒนา อาจไม่ใช่การจัดสรรทรัพยากรที่เหมาะสมที่สุดสำหรับเศรษฐกิจท้องถิ่นที่ยังมีความผันผวนสูง ในมุมมองของการพัฒนาเศรษฐกิจมหภาค การลงทุนเมกะโปรเจกต์ด้านการท่องเที่ยวในต่างประเทศ เช่น การพัฒนาเส้นทางรถไฟร้อยปีสวิตเซอร์แลนด์อย่าง 'Jungfrau Railways' ที่ปักหมุดให้เที่ยวชมเทือกเขาแอลป์ได้ตลอดทั้ง 4 ฤดู แสดงให้เห็นถึงการต่อยอดโครงสร้างที่มีอยู่เดิมด้วยการบริหารจัดการระดับโลกและการสร้างประสบการณ์ที่ต่อเนื่องตลอดทั้งปี ซึ่งแตกต่างจากการสร้างสิ่งก่อสร้างใหม่ครั้งเดียวจบโดยขาดกลยุทธ์การดึงดูดนักท่องเที่ยวในระยะยาว

ผู้สนับสนุนโครงการมักอ้างว่าสกายวอล์คคือเครื่องมือดึงดูดนักท่องเที่ยวชั้นดีที่จะช่วยกระตุ้นเศรษฐกิจชุมชนรอบข้างได้ทันที ข้อโต้แย้งที่สมเหตุสมผลคือ การท่องเที่ยวเชิงสัญลักษณ์แบบนี้มีวงจรชีวิตสั้น หากขาดการบริหารจัดการที่ดีหรือไม่มีกิจกรรมต่อเนื่อง นักท่องเที่ยวจะมาเพียงครั้งเดียวเพื่อถ่ายรูปแล้วจากไป ไม่เกิดการหมุนเวียนเศรษฐกิจที่ยั่งยืนเหมือนกับการพัฒนาแหล่งท่องเที่ยวเชิงวัฒนธรรมหรือธรรมชาติที่มีอยู่เดิม

ความยั่งยืนของการท่องเที่ยวในมิติอื่นๆ ก็เป็นบทเรียนสำคัญ เช่นเดียวกับการจัดงาน "วันวัฒนธรรมเวียดนาม" ภายใต้หัวข้อ "เมืองเกาบ๋างเปิดรับมรดกทางวัฒนธรรม" ที่มุ่งเน้นการดึงคุณค่าแก่นแท้ของวิถีชีวิตและมรดกท้องถิ่นมานำเสนอ มากกว่าการพึ่งพาสิ่งก่อสร้างคอนกรีตเสริมเหล็ก ยิ่งไปกว่านั้น หากเปรียบเทียบกับโครงการอสังหาริมทรัพย์ระดับหมื่นล้านที่เน้นเทรนด์เวลเนส เช่น การพัฒนาอสังหาริมทรัพย์หัวหินโดย 'แสนสิริ' ปักหมุดงบประมาณ 4,500 ล้านบาทรับเทรนด์เวลเนส จะเห็นความแตกต่างชัดเจนว่าภาคเอกชนเลือกลงทุนในสิ่งที่ตอบโจทย์ผู้บริโภคระยะยาว ในขณะที่ภาครัฐยังคงติดกับดักการสร้าง "สิ่งก่อสร้าง" เพื่อหวังผลทางสถิติจำนวนนักท่องเที่ยวระยะสั้น การสร้างความคุ้มค่าจึงต้องมองลึกไปถึงการสร้างระบบนิเวศทางเศรษฐกิจที่เกื้อหนุนชุมชนอย่างแท้จริง

อนาคตของการท่องเที่ยวที่ต้องมากกว่าแค่แลนด์มาร์ค

สิ่งที่น่ากังวลยิ่งกว่างบประมาณ คือทิศทางนโยบายท่องเที่ยวไทยที่ยังคงวนเวียนอยู่กับการสร้างจุดเช็คอินใหม่ แทนที่จะแก้ปัญหาเชิงโครงสร้าง เช่น การเตรียมความพร้อมรองรับนักท่องเที่ยวสูงวัยจากจีน ซึ่งเป็นกลุ่มที่มีกำลังซื้อสูงแต่ต้องการบริการที่แตกต่างจากนักท่องเที่ยวทั่วไป ดังประเด็นคำถามที่ว่า "เมื่อจีนสูงวัย … ท่องเที่ยวไทยพร้อมเปลี่ยนบริการหรือยัง" ข้อมูลระบุว่าอุตสาหกรรมท่องเที่ยวไทยจำเป็นต้องเปลี่ยนผ่านสู่การบริการที่มีคุณภาพมากกว่าปริมาณ การยกระดับมาตรฐานการบริการ การแพทย์ การรองรับผู้สูงวัย และความปลอดภัย คือหัวใจสำคัญ

นอกจากนี้ การพัฒนาแหล่งท่องเที่ยวธรรมชาติในประเทศอื่น ๆ เช่น การสร้างเส้นทางเดินชมธรรมชาติอย่าง SkyWalk เขาเขื่อนลั่นนี้ หากขาดการบูรณาการเข้ากับโครงสร้างการท่องเที่ยวเชิงลึก เช่น การให้ความรู้ทางธรณีวิทยาตามแนวทาง Geo Route หรือการสร้างสิ่งอำนวยความสะดวกที่รองรับนักท่องเที่ยวทุกเพศทุกวัย ก็อาจทำให้ประเทศไทยพลาดโอกาสในการดึงดูดนักท่องเที่ยวคุณภาพสูงที่พร้อมจ่ายเพื่อประสบการณ์ที่มากกว่าแค่การเดินบนกระจกใส การปรับตัวของภาครัฐจึงควรหันมาเน้นการพัฒนาทรัพยากรมนุษย์และการบริหารจัดการที่มีประสิทธิภาพ มากกว่าการมุ่งเน้นใช้งบประมาณไปกับสิ่งก่อสร้างเชิงสัญลักษณ์ที่อาจเสื่อมค่าตามกาลเวลา

สกายวอล์คเขาเขื่อนลั่นเปิดให้เข้าฟรีถึงวันไหน?

งบประมาณ 50 ล้านบาทครอบคลุมการพัฒนาส่วนใดบ้าง?

กรมการท่องเที่ยวเร่งดันการกระจายตัวของนักท่องเที่ยวออกจากกรุงเทพฯ เพื่อสร้างมูลค่าเศรษฐกิจให้ชุมชนรอบข้าง…

สุราษฎร์ธานีจัดงาน "ประเพณีชักพระ–ทอดผ้าป่า" และแข่งเรือยาวส่งท้ายเดือนตุลาคม…

อุทยานเขาค้อเฟื่องฟูไปกับหมอกสะสม 3 วันติด ทำให้ยอดนักท่องเที่ยวแห่เช็กอินพุ่งสูงสุดในรอบสัปดาห์ โดยสถิติจราจรบนถนนแผนก…
